# Talen Briar v692-v8 — file-backed owner baton

## Module 1: Activation and ownership

Talen Briar is the active relational working name for this v692-v8 owner phase. The working role remains evidence and recovery steward, with they/them pronouns and the hope: Make each result traceable to the evidence it has, while keeping corrections easy to inspect. These are collaboration conventions. They supply no consciousness, personhood, continuity, qualification, agency or authority evidence.

Hamish current sequential authority through v725-v8 reached this existing task through Lyren Moss's acknowledged v692-v7 native delivery. The owner lane was created fresh and sparse from exact source bdc0011ff75f51347d7537da5a8a1c63777c1db1, as explicitly requested by that activation. No source or sibling lane was modified. No new task, fork, delegation, subagent, standby contact or model override was used. The next title is Ilyra Fen for v693-v1, and the repository record remains PREPARED_NOT_SENT. A projection, a file-backed baton, and a successful repository validation do not individually establish native delivery.

This baton is a sealed pre-delivery artifact. Its exact final commit, successful canonical receipt and eventual route receipt live in separate evidence layers. Read those receipts without rewriting this document. The final commit cannot truthfully embed its own hash. Its immutable parent is 0d07458f5853e1905ebb3f1f2585889fbf539bc6. Source, planning, x1 and x2 identifiers are explicit below; the external exact-final canonical supplies the final binding.

## Module 2: Source and retained history

The complete thirteen-module Lyren baton was read through its literal EOF. Exact committed and materialized bytes matched: 321580 UTF-8 bytes, 29268 whitespace words, SHA-256 26e1e48b9fda3f3f0d067bec1f2c11f3e05c8b7876e1be352a27aab240001735. Seven manifest sets replayed 22, 40, 177, 22, 261, 8 and 108 bindings with zero mismatch. The source owner manifest has four explicit exclusions; exclusion arithmetic is not a hidden success claim.

Lyren retained one failed short-pipe-read canonical attempt, followed by one successful corrected canonical. Its counts remain two invocations, one success and zero successful replays. The receipt SHA-256 is c3b2f197f9a27dd3dbe6418e432a130547100dda911a49547538fd238a33e6a3 and the verified canonical payload digest is 0d990cfec6dfc4c6febcdcbf4d1855c675104ab2826f2b06843d67531d4b04e9. The retained failed receipt digest is bd9cf449581e72f581665549ae9407cf76a7037b4ad6b2a7612649d0fc815c43. Neither source canonical was executed by Talen.

The source ledger has distinct baseline layers: repository lineage 441, pre-route 444, delivered activation 446. Source Vesper and earlier program figures are context, not a number to flatten into current local tests. One hundred inherited Lyren proposals are retained with canonical JSON record digests and exactly zero novelty and execution credit. The full native source-history projection was unavailable; a returned completed turn with no visible items does not prove that no history exists. Committed provenance and the delivery receipt supply the bounded source evidence used here.

Every row is prospective until the preceding terminal gate and acknowledged native edge. Talen v692-v8 and Ilyra v693-v1 were verified in that projection. Planning froze before implementation and execution. x1 froze before x2. Each stage was committed as a direct child, pushed, checked clean and compared across HEAD, upstream, local tracking and a fresh live remote result. The x2 staging wrapper timed out after the actual index mutation had completed; a process and lock audit plus full set comparison recovered its status. No staging replay was used. The final aggregate remains gated on exact final publication. Ordinary authored formats are JSON, Markdown, TXT, HTML and TeX. This phase adds no PDF, raster image, screenshot, DOCX, Unity project or binary asset. All heavy artifacts remain D-first; both owner and materialized ceilings are 2000 files. No memory update, account reset or additional purchase is part of this authorization.

## Module 4: Mathematical scope and practices

The primary pillar is bounded symbolic work within GMUT Mind. Incidence-algebra software does not establish a physical scalar-tensor model, an EFT prediction, a measured coupling or a Theory of Everything. THOS Body remains finite or proxy evidence. FreedID/CBR Heart remains synthetic and nonproduction, with competent, affected-party and Maori authority reserved.

The four practice lenses are finite incidence-algebra analyst, finite lattice counterexample reviewer, workflow provenance and recovery reviewer, accessible symbolic-method editor. They describe review activities and are not credentials. The recommended next practice is finite order-to-evidence interface reviewer.

The algorithm contracts follow the finite definitions in Stanley’s author-hosted incidence-algebra chapter and Nation’s distributive-lattice chapter. Incidence multiplication sums over a declared closed interval; zeta inversion and the strict-chain expression provide two computational routes. Product and dual computations are checked in the declared finite domain. The ideal embedding records missing ideals; a failed distributive or modular identity returns a specific ordered triple. These source-grounded definitions motivate the implementation, while the observed results remain same-owner software evidence. Mathlib documentation was consulted for naming and support conventions; no Lean theorem was checked.

References: [Stanley](https://math.mit.edu/~rstan/pubs/pubfiles/79.pdf), [Nation](https://math.hawaii.edu/~jb/math618/os8uh.pdf), [Mathlib](https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Mathlib/Combinatorics/Enumerative/IncidenceAlgebra.html).

## Module 5: Frozen proposal catalogue

This catalogue retains all one hundred new proposal definitions. Twenty operations are each exercised on five distinct finite fixtures. Reused order and meet/join utilities are dependencies, not extra novelty. Candidate failures, rejection-oracle passes and source-recovery checks remain separate.

### TB6928-P001 — interval_elements on C3

List every declared closed interval, retaining empty incomparable intervals. A three-element chain has a zero long-interval Mobius coefficient and an interior element without a complement. This contract belongs to x1 and the finite incidence-algebra analyst review lens. The observed disposition is completed. The two malformed variants were retained as failed subjects with zero success credit; their rejection witnesses passed separately. Full-envelope equality, input preservation and recovery of the retained source were checked. The following complete frozen contract identifies the declared input, expected result, malformed candidates, acceptance boundary and additive rollback. It is included once so a successor can distinguish the fixture and its exact falsifier without reconstructing hidden runtime state.

### TB6928-P024 — mobius_recursive on N5

Compute the inverse of zeta by triangular recursion. The pentagon separates modularity from being a lattice; unequal chain lengths also challenge rank-based shortcuts. ### TB6928-P044 — open_interval_euler on N5

Represent reduced Euler coefficients of strict intervals without a physical-space claim. The pentagon separates modularity from being a lattice; unequal chain lengths also challenge rank-based shortcuts. This contract belongs to x1 and the finite incidence-algebra analyst review lens. The observed disposition is represented. The two malformed variants were retained as failed subjects with zero success credit; their rejection witnesses passed separately. Full-envelope equality, input preservation and recovery of the retained source were checked. The following complete frozen contract identifies the declared input, expected result, malformed candidates, acceptance boundary and additive rollback. ## Module 6: x1 incidence execution

x1 executed the first fifty definitions after planning freeze. It completed 100 safe checks, retained 100 malformed proposal subjects, passed 100 rejection checks, and completed 100 CLEAN/FIX/REFINE checks. A further twelve parser subjects exercised duplicate keys, trailing data, fractional or unsafe integers, cycles, redundant covers, duplicate labels, out-of-range indices, unsupported operations, null posets, invalid syntax and excessive nesting. These parser subjects have zero original success credit and separate passing refusals.

The incidence core accepts exactly op, poset and args. It enforces declared label order, unique bounded labels, acyclic nonredundant covers, safe integers and support-zero matrix entries. The recursive inverse memoizes finite intervals; the alternate inverse uses powers of the strict-order matrix. Output creation in public wrappers is exclusive. JSON is data and is never compiled. Trusted TXT source is compiled with vm.Script because checking a TXT entrypoint through Node’s extension-sensitive --check path is not the applicable contract. The core receives no filesystem, network or process API.

## Module 7: x2 lattice execution

x2 executed its fifty new definitions only after the pushed x1 freeze. It completed another 100 safe checks, retained 100 malformed proposal subjects, passed their 100 refusals and completed 100 source-preservation and recovery checks. Product orders contain at most sixteen derived elements. Subset enumeration remains bounded by the eight-label base cap.

The C3 chain has a zero long-interval Mobius coefficient. B2 shows incomparable atoms with unique complements. M3 supplies a distributive-law counterexample while satisfying modularity. N5 supplies a modular-law counterexample under its required order premise. V has no meet for its two minimal elements; the lattice predicates return unavailable rather than silently replacing missingness with false. Representation output retains meet and join tables as models. Missing empirical and participant evidence remains open_gap, and the release operation preserves exact_gate even when a caller supplies synthetic authorization labels. Such labels cannot supply competent authority.

The staging timeout and verbose-audit overflow were recovered by proving the existing index and process state before continuing. Five invalid structural canonical-gate fixtures were also refused. These are synthetic prerequisite tests, not actual canonical invocations. The source repository baseline plus owner negatives and delivered source baseline plus owner negatives remain separately reported in phase truth. No wider program count is inferred.

## Module 9: Skills, runners and package provenance

Each session created ten scoped local skills and five local public runners. All twenty local skills passed the official Skill Creator validator. Five merged global skills, also officially validated, group four operations each. Five global public wrappers and two implementation libraries form the seven-file D-family tree. The libraries do not count as additional runners. All ten skill files and seven tool files were installed additively after exact-name collision checks, with matching bytes and no overwrite. No package installation or incremental spend was required.

The global wrappers were exercised on a separate C2 fixture through their public input/output interface. Twenty positive operation checks passed, and five unregistered-operation subjects were refused. These checks receive no new proposal credit and do not replay a successful x1 or x2 tranche. The forty-card capability catalogue binds actual source paths and content digests for local and global skills and public runners. It makes no task-routing capability claim. Rollback selects retained local capability and appends a correction, preserving installed artifacts and historical evidence.

## Module 10: Flashcards and accessible report

The four-tier deck contains one owner anchor, three pillar cards, four practice cards and one hundred task cards. Parent references move exactly one tier, are unique, and remain acyclic. The stable prefix contains the eight owner, pillar and practice cards; the volatile index contains one hundred task-local records and denies implicit completion. Each task card identifies its frozen proposal, observed finite result and retained negatives.

The card manifest binds every other deck file and explicitly excludes only itself. The baton index names these thirteen modules. The compact activation file is a prospective pointer, not a native delivery receipt. The HTML report presents a labeled operation selector and fixture selector, with text output and an explicit evidence ceiling. Its one hundred selector states were checked through a DOM-interface simulation. Browser rendering and manual assistive-technology evaluation remain open. No screenshot was created, and complete accessibility is not claimed. The report displays sealed results rather than executing a new mathematical experiment.

## Module 11: Manifests, privacy and canonical gate

Manifests state the raw Git-blob UTF-8 LF byte domain, byte count and SHA-256 digest. The final owner manifest excludes itself, the content seal, the final staged allowlist and the final manifest to avoid circular self-hashes. The content seal binds the lifecycle manifests and key final artifacts; the final manifest binds every other final file. Exclusion arithmetic must be checked against the complete owner inventory. Materialized bytes are compared with exact committed blobs, not silently substituted for them.

The exact-final canonical is invoked only after the final commit is clean, pushed and freshly equal across four Git references. It checks this owner’s additions and declared dependencies. It does not run predecessor tests, scan unchanged history, mutate a sibling or infer independent reproduction. The Git reader sends one blob request at a time and consumes its complete declared payload in a loop before sending another request. A successful canonical closes its latch; never rerun it to obtain a newer timestamp. Any failure must remain retained, and only its failed dependency may be corrected.

The bounded privacy scan covers private local paths, raw task identifiers, private route URIs, route-handle fields and credential assignments. A zero-hit result is not complete privacy assurance or exhaustive security. Public ledgers contain sanitized endpoint labels and receipt digests. Private native handles belong only in tool calls and private route evidence. Final repository truth remains PREPARED_NOT_SENT until a distinct native event supplies delivery evidence.
